PUDC-23-184 
 
 
January 8, 2024 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The property is adjacent to the City to the west, north, and east and is part of a 
County pocket. The contiguity to the City and the proximity to surrounding urban-style 
development makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The development of this 
property will benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic 
fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Utilities exist in the area and will be expanded into the 
proposed area as it develops. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 94.82% contiguous, along all but the southern edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on December 5, 2023. The 
public hearing for this annexation is scheduled for January 8, 2024. Twenty (20) business days 
(on the City’s calendar) prior to the announced public hearing is December 5, 2023. The earliest 
confirmation of delivery was dated December 7, 2023. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 

PUDC-23-184 
 
 
January 8, 2024 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
Policies within PlanCheyenne encourage the annexation of County pockets. This is part of a County 
Pocket.    
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with urban residential uses. These areas are expected to develop with full access 
to urban services – such areas are best served by being within City limits. The area is within the Urban 
Service Boundary. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Application 
4. Applicant-submitted annexation map

customarily available to residents of the city or town

Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment including land 
being annexed to the city.  
 
 
Section 3.  That on January 2, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
public hearings, reviewed the proposed zoning assignment that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zoning assignment.  
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning 
assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 5.  That in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as AG 
Agricultural and LR Low-density Residential. 
 
 
Section 6.   That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend 
the Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 7.   Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8.   That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
 
PUDC-23-184: The Bluff’s 12th Filing – Assigned Zoning 
LOCATION: 
   East of Converse Avenue and south of Storey Boulevard. 
REQUEST: 
  Assigning the AG and LR zone to property that has petitioned for 
annexation. 
ANNEXATION 
APPLICANT: 
   Randy Guill 
OWNER: 
Horizon Development, LLC 
ZONING: 
County A-1 to City AG and County LR to City LR 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential  
PURPOSE: 
To establish a City zoning districts concurrent with annexation. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-23-184 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning of Land to be known as the 
Bluff’s 12th Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming to AG Agriculture and LR Low-density Residential as shown 
in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The applicant has petitioned for land located east of Converse Avenue and south of Storey Boulevard to be 
annexed into city limits. Annexation of a parcel requires a city zone to be established. Per UDC 2.2.8 a zone 
will be assigned based on the underlying county zone and per the policy document adopted by the city. 
Should the applicant wish to have a different zone at the time of annexation, they will be required to submit 
a subsequent zoning map amendment, proposing a different zone.  
 
The applicant has submitted a zoning map amendment proposing the CB Community Business and MR 
Medium-density Residential zone. The application is PUDC-23-181.   
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The underlying county zones are A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential and LR Low-density 
Residential. The most analogous city zones are AG Agricultural and LR Low-density Residential 
according to the policy document. The Assigned Zoning policy document is attached to the staff 
report as attachment number three.  
 
Establishment of the City zone matches the policy document; therefore, the item complies with 
review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
 
 

PUDC-23-184 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning of land to be known as the Bluff’s 12th Filing, Laramie 
County, Wyoming to AG Agricultural and LR Low-density Residential as shown in the attached zone 
change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
 
1. Zoning Map 
2. Annexation Map 
3. Assigned Zoning Policy Document

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM AG AGRICULTURAL AND LR LOW-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL 
TO CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS AND MR MEDIUM-DENSITY 
RESIDENTIAL FOR LAND LOCATED EAST OF CONVERSE AVENUE 
AND SOUTH OF STOREY BOULEVARD." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment ("zone change") for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, 
Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That on January 2, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lot 1, Block 1, The Bluffs, 12th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed to 
CB Community Business; and zoning classification of the real property described as for Lot 1, 
Block 2; Lot 1, Block 3; and Tract A, of The Bluffs, 12th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby 
changed to MR Medium-density Residential.  
 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon the official filing of the affiliated The Bluffs, 12th Filing final plat map with 
the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

PUDC-23-181: The Bluffs 12th Filing – Zoning Map Amendment 
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 29.61 acres of land from AG and LR 
to CB and MR. 
LOCATION: 
East of Polk Avenue and north of and adjacent to E Pershing Blvd. 
APPLICANT: 
Randy Guill 
AGENT: 
Randy Guill 
OWNER: 
Horizon Development, LLC 
ZONING: 
AG Agricultural and LR Low Density Residential 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential 
PURPOSE: 
To allow development of a church and future residential development. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-23-181 
 
 
January 2, 2023 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the rezoning of land south of Storey Blvd. and east of 
Converse Avenue, to be known as the Bluffs 12th Filing from AG and LR to CB and MR as shown in the 
attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from AG and LR to CB and MR for approximately 
29.61 acres of land. This property is proposed to be annexed and will be assigned the AG and LR zone based 
on the underlying County zone. The applicant has requests it be rezoned to CB and MR to allow development 
of a church at the intersection of Converse Avenue and Storey Blvd. and allow for the rest of the land to be 
developed with residential at a later date.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. 
 
Future Land Use Designation

PUDC-23-181 
 
 
January 2, 2023 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential. The surrounding area is 
predominantly the same. According to the comprehensive plan, the optimal City zone for the 
subject property’s future land use designation is any of the residential zone districts. A large 
portion of this area is proposed to be rezoned to MR Medium-density Residential which would 
fall in line with the future land use designation.  
 
The applicant has proposed a future land use amendment for a portion of the land near the 
intersection of Storey Blvd. and Converse Avenue. Their proposed amendment would change 
the area to CB Community Business and their proposed zone change for that same area is to CB 
Community Business which is best suited for that land designation.     
 
The policies and future land use map of PlanCheyenne are supportive of this proposed zone 
changes. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Surrounding properties south of Storey are zoned MR, NR-3 and P. Much of the development in 
this area is residential and the public zone is a school which supports the adjacent residential. 
The proposed MR zone would fit with the rest of the residential in the area and the proposed CB 
zone at the intersection would create an area that would support the residential with a service.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property is proposed to be annexed into city limits and will be given the AG and LR zone. 
The applicant has requested to change the zone to CB and MR. The CB zone would allow for 
commercial uses, but there is a proposed church development for that area. The rest of the 
property would be zoned to MR which would allow for a greater variety of residential uses than 
the LR zone or the AG zone.   
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The proposed area to be rezoned is also associated with an Expedited Plat (PUDC-23-183) which 
proposes additional rights-of-way and connections that will allow for the area to develop as 
proposed with this zone change. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 

PUDC-23-181 
 
 
January 2, 2023 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The MR District is intended for a mix of density and dwelling types within a neighborhood. All 
uses require full access to public infrastructure and city services. The regulations are designed to 
protect the neighborhood character of the district and protect the primarily residential use of 
land. The property has access to all public infrastructure and city services. The proposed use will 
meet the intent of the proposed zoning. The MR District is applicable to areas where more 
compact neighborhood development patterns are desired, and specifically the Urban Residential 
Category in the Cheyenne Comprehensive Plan. The area proposed to be zoned MR meets the 
intent and applicability for the proposed zone district.  
 
The CB District is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that may serve a 
number of residential neighborhoods or other districts in meeting daily needs, discretionary 
needs, or other business functions. The district regulations are designed to encourage these 
uses, while also protecting the abutting and nearby areas. The CB District is applicable in areas 
where moderate- or large-scale business uses are appropriate, specifically larger Mixed-use 
Commercial Activity Centers, Community/Regional Activity Centers, or Community Business 
areas of the Comprehensive Plan. The area proposed to be zoned CB meets the intent and with 
the proposed future land use map amendment would meet the applicability for the proposed 
zone district.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The proposed use for the CB zone is a church facility. This use is permitted in the CB zone, and it 
will be able to meet all applicable UDC standards for this lot. The CB zone would allow other 
commercial, or service type uses and those uses would be able to meet the standards of this 
zone district as it is a vacant parcel and would be constructed from the ground up. Having the 
property under City jurisdiction would allow the property to access City services. 
 
The area proposed to be MR has no proposed development. The MR zone would allow for the 
land to develop with a mix of residential uses. Those uses would be able to meet the standards 
of this zone district as it is a vacant parcel and would be constructed from the ground up. Having 
the property under City jurisdiction would allow the property to access City services. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 

PUDC-23-181 
 
 
January 2, 2023 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d). The CB and MR 
zone is appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and surrounding 
development patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from AG and LR to CB and MR. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM
NB NEIGHBORHOOD BUSINESS TO CB
COMMUNITY 
BUSINESS FOR LAND LOCATED SOUTHEAST OF AND ADJACENT TO 
THE 
INTERSECTION 
OF 
DELL 
RANGE 
BOULEVARD 
AND 
BLUEGRASS CIRCLE." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC").
Section 2. That on January 2, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lots 1 and 2, Block 7, Meadowbrooke Park, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed to 
CB Community Business.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

FILE:

Neighborhood Business to CB Community Business.
LOCATION:
Southeast of the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Bluegrass 
Circle
AGENT:
Christofer Lovas

RTB Dell Range LTD LLC

NB Neighborhood Business

PURPOSE:

PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-23-189
January 2, 2024
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lots 1 and 2, Block 7, Meadowbrooke Park to CB Community Business, 
noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from NB Neighborhood Business to CB 
Community Business. The land is currently occupied by a restaurant without a drive through. The purpose of 
the proposed zone change is to allow for the construction of a new drive-through restaurant with more 
appropriate design standards.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
The Future Land Use designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial. 
Commercial category is intended to promote a range of land uses, with primarily retail, office, 
light industrial, and live/work 
, plazas and/or open space 
should also be part of 
 
 
Policies in the comprehensive plan encourage commercial development near activity centers, 
and the intersection of College and Pershing is considered an activity center by the plan.

UDC-23-189
January 2, 2024
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion.
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
There is CB zoning to all but the south of this property. The surrounding area consists of a mix of 
different restaurant, retail, and residential uses. The CB zone would be compatible with the 
nearby properties as it is the commercial zone of all neighboring non-residential properties.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The surrounding area better matches the expected development patterns of CB zoning. The NB 
zone requires buildings to be set closer to the streets and have a certain amount of windows 
and other building design elements, whereas the CB zone pushes buildings further from the 
street and requires fewer building design elements. The CB zone is also meant for more vehicle-
oriented areas which aptly describes development along Dell Range. The NB zone is intended to 
integrate with residential neighborhoods and has much fewer allowed commercial uses than the 
CB zone. Due to surrounding CB zoning, there 
 need to integrate with a residential 
neighborhood and limit uses at this location. 
   
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
This is in an area with an existing street network, and no changes to the network are 
anticipated. The CB zone generally anticipates larger blocks and the existing block meets block 
size standards.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB District is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that may serve a 
number of residential neighborhoods or other districts in meeting daily needs, discretionary 
needs, or other business functions. The district regulations are designed to encourage these 
uses, while also protecting the abutting and nearby areas.  
 
The CB District is applicable in areas where moderate- or large-scale business uses are 
appropriate, specifically larger Mixed-use Commercial Activity Centers, Community/Regional 
Activity Centers, or Community Business areas of the Comprehensive Plan. 
 

UDC-23-189
January 2, 2024
The property is along the Dell Range corridor with a regional activity center designation and a 
large Community Business designated area immediately to the north.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific proposal for this project is a food service use (restaurant) with a drive-through. 
However, if that project is not executed after approval, the CB zone offers a wide variety of 
potential auto-oriented uses at this site (including apartments).   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No major concerns were raised by other revieing bodies. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The proposed 
CB zone is consistent with the comprehensive plan and is appropriate based on the existing conditions 
and potential development patterns for the area.  
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-23-189
January 2, 2024
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from NB to CB.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS TO CB COMMUNITY 
BUSINESS FOR LAND LOCATED NORTH OF AND ADJACENT TO 
PERSHING 
BOULEVARD 
BETWEEN

AND

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC").
Section 2. That on January 2, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as: A portion of Tract 23, Sunnyside Addition, 2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming, more particularly described as follows: Tract 23, Sunnyside Addition, 2nd Filing less 
(42,868 sq. ft., bk 1554 pg 1220) a portion desc as: beginning at the northeast corner of said tract 
23; thence on an azimuth of 179 deg 59' 27", along the east line of said Tract 23, a distance of 
299.24'; thence 272 deg 08' 34", a distance of 150.05' to the west line of said Tract 23; thence 359 
deg 59' 41", along said west line, a distance of 283.60' to the northwest corner of said Tract 23; 
thence 90 deg 22' 16", along the north line of said Tract 23, a distance of 149.92' to the point of 
beginning, is hereby changed to CB Community Business.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with

FILE:
UDC-23-190: Christian Brothers Automotive Zone Change 
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 1.1 acres of land from
MUB Mixed-use Business to CB Community Business.
LOCATION:
North of Pershing Boulevard between McCann Avenue and North 
College Drive
AGENT:
OWNER:
Brad Kuehl
Karens Properties LLC
ZONING:
MUB Mixed-use Business
FUTURE LAND USE:
Mixed-use Commercial
PURPOSE:
Rezoning to CB to allow automobile repair with fewer design standards (and 
without conditional use).
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-23-190
January 2, 2024
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of a portion of Tract 23, Sunnyside Addition, 2nd Filing to CB Community 
Business, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MUB Mixed-use Business to CB Community 
Business. The land is currently vacant. The purpose of the proposed zone change is to allow for an 
automobile repair use with more appropriate design standards.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
The Future Land Use designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial. 
Commercial category is intended to promote a range of land uses, with primarily retail, office, 
light industrial, and live/work 
 plazas and/or open space 
should also be part of 
 
 
Policies in the comprehensive plan encourage commercial development near activity centers, 
and the intersection of College and Pershing is considered an activity center by the plan.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Mixed-use Commercial  
MUB Mixed-use Business

North 
Mixed-use Commercial

Business

Assorted commercial 
service uses

Commercial service, carwash

UDC-23-190
January 2, 2024
2)
The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
There is CB zoning to the northeast of this property. The surrounding area consists of a mix of 
different commercial, limited industrial, assembly, and service uses. The CB zone would be 
compatible with the nearby properties as it is a commercial zone with slightly different 
standards than the MUB zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The surrounding area north of Pershing better matches the expected development patterns of 
CB zoning. The MUB zone is intended to be more pedestrian-oriented and requires buildings to 
be set closer to the streets and have a certain amount of windows and other building design 
elements, whereas the CB zone pushes buildings further from the street and requires fewer 
building design elements. The specifically proposed use (automobile repair) is an auto-centric 
business and is a use by right in the CB zone and a conditional use in the MUB zone. The CB zone 
and MUB zone share a lot of similarities in allowed uses. 
   
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
This is in an area with an existing street network which meets the block standards for the CB 
zone district (but not the current MUB zone district). No platting actions creating new streets 
are expected.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB District is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that may serve a 
number of residential neighborhoods or other districts in meeting daily needs, discretionary 
needs, or other business functions. The district regulations are designed to encourage these 
uses, while also protecting the abutting and nearby areas.  
 
The CB District is applicable in areas where moderate- or large-scale business uses are 
appropriate, specifically larger Mixed-use Commercial Activity Centers, Community/Regional 
Activity Centers, or Community Business areas of the Comprehensive Plan. 
 

UDC-23-190
January 2, 2024
This property is close to College and Pershing and existing development (including current 
blocks) in the area have a more spread-out development pattern, better meeting the Intent and 
design objectives of the CB zone, than those of the MUB zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific proposal for this project is an automobile repair facility. However, if that project is 
not executed after approval, the CB zone offers a wide variety of potential auto-oriented uses at 
this site (including apartments).   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No major concerns were raised by other revieing bodies. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The proposed 
CB zone is consistent with the comprehensive plan and is appropriate based on the existing conditions 
and potential development patterns for the area.  
 
 
 
 

UDC-23-190
January 2, 2024
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MUB to CB.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-01-02_TotalExtraction_51249120.pdf
Telephone: (307) 637-6306 | Facsimile: (307) 637-6373 | Website: www.cheyennecity.org 
 
Page 1 of 1 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Staff Report 
 
TO: Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne 
FROM: Stefanie Boster, Cheyenne City Attorney 
DATE: 1/71/2024 
RE: City Councilmember Compensation 
 
Wyoming law provides that the salaries of all officers including the salaries of the Mayor and each 
councilman shall be fixed by ordinance.  Wyo. Stat. § 15-3-205(a)(i,ii).  The statute further 
provides that “[a]ll other officers of the city shall receive the compensation fixed by ordinance 
except as otherwise provided by law.”  Wyo. Stat. § 15-3-205(b).  The term “salary” and 
“compensation” are not defined in this ordinance and appear to be used interchangeably under this 
statute.  
 
In the City of Cheyenne, ordinance is fixed by a non-codified ordinance. Changes to the most 
recent ordinance, Ordinance No. 4431, was published on May and effective on January 1, 2023.  
Prior to this change, Ordinance No. 3914 regarding City Councilmember Compensation was 
published in March of 2011 and Ordinance No. 3419 was published on December 30, 2000. All 
three most recent ordinance fix the compensation of councilman, not salary.  The attached 
proposed ordinance adds the word “salary” specifically for “constituent services” to the ordinance. 
 
Notably, while the salary of members of the City Council can be changed by ordinance, it cannot 
be effective during the officials’ term.  Wyo. Const. Art. 3, § 32; Art. 14 § 1; Wyo. Stat. § 15-3-
205(b).  The Wyoming Constitution provides in pertinent part that, “[n]o law shall extend the term 
of any public officer or increase or diminish his salary or emolument after his election or 
appointment …” Wyo. Const. Art. 3, § 32.   
 
The Wyoming Supreme Court has interpreted the language in Art. 3, § 32 to mean that, “[w]hen it 
says that the salary of a public officer shall not be increased or diminished after his election, it 
means that the salary or compensation for the term to which he was elected shall not be changed 
after, but becomes fixed as of the date of the election.”  Nickerson v. Winslow, 22 Wyo. 259, 268-
269, 138 P. 184, 186, rehg denied 22 Wyo. 259, 140 P. 834 (1914), quoted in Board of County 
Com’rs of County of Platte v. State ex rel Yeadon, 971 P.2d 129 (Wyo. 1998). 
 
Thus, pursuant to the Wyoming Constitution, state statute and relevant case law, changes to the 
ordinance regarding City Council Member compensation will not impact current members of the 
City Council. 
 
 
CITY ATTORNEY’S OFFICE 
Municipal Building 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 308 
Cheyenne WY 82001 

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING ORDINANCE NO. 4431, WHICH 
ESTABLISHED A NEW COMPENSATION SCHEDULE FOR MEMBERS 
OF THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, TO CLARIFY 
THE MANNER IN WHICH COMPENSATION IS TO BE COMPUTED 
AND PROVIDING FOR AN EFFECTIVE DATE." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Ordinance No. 4431 is amended as follows: 
 
 
Section 2.  Compensation. 
 
A. Members of the City Council shall receive either compensation, or a salary or for 
constituent services to offset expenses, in the amount Two Thousand Dollars ($2,000.00) 
a month.  Council Members may elect to decline any portion of the compensation or salary. 
 
B. In the event a City Council Member does not hold office for an entire month, he or 
she’stheir salary or compensation will be compensated on a per diem calculated on a pro 
rata basis ($2,000.00 divided by the number of days in the month, multiplied by the number 
of days in office during that month).  For the first day and the last day that the member 
holds office, he or she shall be entitled to one-half of the per diem daily rate. 
 
C.  Cheyenne City Council Members will be compensated for non-local travel on City related 
business according to established City policy applicable to City employees. 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 4.  This Ordinance will be in full force and effect on January 1, 2025, and will 
apply to City Council Members whose terms commence in 2025 and thereafter.

__________________________________________ 
PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR

KRISTINA F. JONES, CITY CLERK

adopted by the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne by Resolution No. 5573, is the Master 
Plan for the City of Cheyenne and the Comprehensive Plan for portions of Laramie County adopted 
by both the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County Board of County 
Commissioners; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the intent of the Future Land Use plan is not to predetermine specific land 
uses for individual parcels or specific locations for uses or facilities, but to provide a general 
framework for neighborhoods and activity centers organized around roadway networks and major 
parks and open space; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Taran Triplett of Edwards Development, agent for a property owner within 
Cheyenne, has initiated an amendment to the Future Land Use map to amend the land use category 
from Urban Residential to Community Business for land to be known as Lot 1, Block 1, The 
Bluffs, 12th Filing; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendments approved by the Governing Body pursuant to the criteria 
set forth on Page 18 of PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress, as amended; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Laramie County Board of County Commissioners reviewed the proposed 
future land use map amendment and were given the opportunity to make comments; 
 
 
WHEREAS, a public hearing to receive public input and comments on the proposed Future 
Land Use map amendment for the above-described property was held before the City of Cheyenne 
Planning Commission on January 2, 2024 and the Commission approved the proposed Future Land 
Use map amendment; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendment processes and procedures required by PlanCheyenne, as 
amended, have been followed with regard to the request by Taran Triplett to amend the Future 
Land Use map category for the above-described property. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne Governing Body hereby certifies 
Planning Commission action amending the Land Use Plan for PlanCheyenne changing the land 
use category from Urban Residential to Community Business, in accordance with the procedures 
outlined in PlanCheyenne and Wyoming Statutes § 15-1-503 and § 15-1-505. 
 
7:45 pm, Jan 02 2024

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that staff is authorized to amend affected maps of 
PlanCheyenne to reflect the aforementioned changes; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this resolution shall become effective after its 
passage, approval and publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024.

JANUARY 2, 2024 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-23-182:  Future Land Use Plan Amendment from UR to CB 
REQUEST: 
A request to amend the Future Land Use Plan from UR (Urban Residential) 
to CB (Community Business). 
LOCATION: 
East of Converse Avenue and south of Storey Boulevard. 
APPLICANT: 
Randy Guill 
OWNER: 
Horizon Development, LLC 
ZONING: 
County A-1, proposed rezoning to CB Community Business following 
annexation 
PURPOSE: 
To match a requested zone change of a portion of the area to CB Community 
Business for development of the property. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-23-182 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Resolution amending the Future Land Use Plan for a portion of land southeast of 
the intersection of Converse Avenue and Storey Boulevard as shown in the Future Land Use Map 
provided from Urban Residential to Community Business.  
    
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
PlanCheyenne (2016 Community Plan) 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting amending the Future Land Use Map for roughly 7.91 acres of land from Urban 
Residential to CB Community Business to accommodate a zone change to permit a large Community 
Assembly.  
 
A concurrent application for a zoning map amendment has been submitted. The zoning map amendment will 
accompany the future land use amendment at City Council. If the application for the zoning map amendment 
is approved, the applicant will be required to submit a site plan application for the new development. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is shown as Urban Residential in the Future Land Use Plan. The Urban Residential category 
includes many of the Cheyenne Area’s existing neighborhoods, as well as planned future neighborhoods 
that will connect to City utilities. A broader variety of residential types, including single-family 
residences, duplexes, patio homes, townhomes, condominiums, and apartments.

PUDC-23-182 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to approve an amendment to the Future Land Use Map of the 
Comprehensive Plan, the Commission must make the following considerations outlined in the 2014 
Community Plan (Build Chapter page 3-3).  A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) BENEFICIAL OR ADDRESSES ISSUES - The proposed amendment is beneficial to the community 
or addresses issues not foreseen during the planning process.    
 
The proposed amendment to CB Community business would be beneficial at the proposed 
location as it is a major intersection for two major streets. Commercial should be located along 
major streets and at major intersections. The proposed use for this area is for a church. Even if 
the church does not develop it would allow for other commercial uses at this location that could 
provide services to the surrounding area. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 1. 
 
2) COMPATIBLY - The proposed amendment is compatible with the surrounding area, and the 
goals and policies of the Plan.  
 
The subject property is adjacent to Urban Residential designations. Secondary uses for the 
urban residential designation allow for places of worship which is the proposed use. The 
commercial designation would allow the same use but also allow this corner to develop with 
additional commercial uses should the church not develop. This designation would be similar to 
other major interactions throughout the community. The proposed amendment is consistent 
with the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne, as discussed in more detail below. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 2. 
   
3) IMPACT MITIGATION - The proposed amendment will address and mitigate impacts on 
transportation, services, and facilities.  
 
Transportation: 
The transportation network in this area of the City is currently developed. All impacts to the 
transportation network will be addressed through the platting and site plan processes. 
Additional rights-of-way are proposed with the proposed plat. 
 
Services: 
The proposed change will not have any adverse effects on the provision of services to the 
subject property. Service providers, such as the fire department, will be included in platting and 
site planning processes to assess service needs. 
 
Facilities: 
The proposed change will likely not require new facilities outside of the development area. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 3. 
 
 
 

PUDC-23-182 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
4) SERVICE PROVISION - The proposed amendment will have minimal effect on service provision, 
including adequacy or availability of urban facilities and services, and is compatible with 
existing and planned service provision  
 
This land is currently proposed to be annexed into City limits and urban facilities and services 
are available to the subject properties. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 4. 
 
5) ANNEXATION - The proposed amendment does not jeopardize the City’s ability to annex the 
property. 
 
The subject property is currently proposed to be annexed into City limits.   
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 5. 
 .   
6) URBAN SERVICES AREA - The proposed amendment is consistent with the logical expansion of 
the Urban Services Area. 
 
This land is currently within the Urban Services Area and does not expand the current area. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 6. 
 
7) STRICT ADHERENCE - Strict adherence to the Plan would result in a situation neither intended 
nor in keeping with other key elements and policies of the Plan. 
 
Strict adherence to the Plan may result in unintended consequences. While the land could 
develop completely with residential, the proposed area is at the intersection of two major 
streets which is better suited for commercial centers and development. By changing the future 
land use for this corner it allows it to develop with high intense uses than residential and keep 
the residential away from a major intersection.  
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 7. 
 
8) PUBLIC WELFARE AND CONSISTENCY – The proposed plan amendment will promote the public 
welfare and will be consistent with the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne and the elements 
thereof. 
 
Staff is satisfied that the proposed application will not have any adverse effects on public 
welfare. Furthermore, the application accomplishes the following policies contained within 
PlanCheyenne: 
 
• 
Policy 1.1.A: Balanced Mix of Land Uses 
The Future Land Use Plan establishes a balanced mix of residential and non-residential 
land uses throughout the Cheyenne Area. The City and County should use the Future 
Land Use Plan and this Plan’s policies as the main tools for guiding decisions about 
growth and development. 
 
 
 

PUDC-23-182 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
• 
Policy 2.2.B: Mix of uses in Neighborhoods 
Incorporate a mix of uses in new neighborhoods including complementary 
nonresidential uses such as neighborhood commercial services, where feasible and 
appropriate, that serve nearby residents and are designed and operated in harmony 
with the residential characteristics of a neighborhood. 
 
• 
Policy 3.3.A: Commercial Development in Designated Activity Centers 
Encourage the concentration of commercial, employment, and other services within 
designated activity centers (Community/Regional Activity Centers, Mixed-Use, and 
Neighborhood Business), as delineated on the Future Land Use Plan. Ensure individual 
uses are aligned with the scale, mix of uses, locational guidance, and desire 
characteristics defined for each type of center in this chapter. Place a particular 
emphasis on the design quality of business development to create vital and lasting 
centers and attractive places. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
No other plans are applicable for this area.    
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff recommends a Community Business Land Use Category for this property because it is 
located at the intersection of two major streets and allows for a proposed church. A future land 
use amendment to Community Business will support future rezoning initiatives in the area that 
permit the development of the land with new developments that will support the adjacent 
neighborhoods.  
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan as submitted by the applicant and 
expanded upon by staff (Staff Recommendation). 
2. Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan subject to modifications. 
3. Deny the Resolution to not amend the Land Use Plan.  The Planning Commission must conclude 
that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings for denial 
must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Resolution until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation, the Commission shall cite specific 
reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan for a portion of land 
southeast of the intersection of Converse Avenue and Storey Boulevard as shown in the applicants 
submitted Future Land Use map from Urban Residential to CB Community Business.      
 

PUDC-23-182 
 
 
January 2, 2024

3. Application

Nov 10, 2023

Petition to Amend Comprehensive Plan Future Land Use Map 
 
 
I, Taran Triplett, of 2505 E 13th Street, Cheyenne, WY 82001 am hereby petitioning to amend the 
Comprehensive Plan specific to a parcel of land located in T14N R66W NW¼ NW¼ Sec 21. The 
parcel in question, known as The Bluffs 12th Filing, is approximately 7.91 acres located at the 
corner of Converse Avenue and Storey Boulevard (see attached Preliminary Plat, Annexation 
Map and Zone Change Map for reference). The area currently falls under the “Mixed-Use 
Residential” future land use type. This petition proposes changing the parcel in question to a 
“Community Business” future land use type. Specifically, the property is currently being 
developed as a future location for a church.  
 
The Unified Development Code (UDC) Article 5 lists the Community Business District (CB) zoning 
as appropriate for Civic uses serving a number of residential neighborhoods. The location of the 
parcel at the corner of two arterial roads lends itself well to access from adjacent 
neighborhoods as well as from more distant areas within the city. The proposed connections of 
Ogden Road and Wild Bluff provide a buffer to minimize impacts on surrounding areas and 
transportation impacts, which is also a goal of the CB zoning. The proposed connections also 
serve to relive traffic pressure on the remaining neighborhood and will benefit future 
development of the area. Should the development of a church not come to fruition, the CB 
future land use type could accommodate a variety of different types of commercial use on the 
corner parcel.  
 
Signed, 
 
 
 
 
 
Taran Triplett

BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for 29.61 acres of land. The subject property is 
unplatted, vacant land, which is concurrently being annexed to the City.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with residential 
uses. The applicant has requested a future land use amendment for Lot 1, Block 1 to become 
Community Business. The plat provides larger lots than you would typically see for residential 
development unless used for multi-family or to be replatted at a later date. The plat does 
propose a commercial lot at a size that you would generally see.

Mixed-use Residential 
NR-3 (Neighborhood 
Residential – High density) Multi-family / Vacant 

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat divides the land into three large parcels and a small remainder parcel. Two of 
the large parcels are proposed to be for residential development and may be replatted in the 
future. The plat does connect Ogden Road to Storey Blvd. to the north and also proposes 
connecting Wild Bluff to Converse to the west.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$8,540 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$8,540 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$17,080.00 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Commercial Enhancement Fee $1,100 per acre 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
The applicant proposes extending Wild Bluff to Converse and Ogden to Storey. These are 
major road connections that we would expect to happen with the proposed plat. Further 
road networks could be proposed with the replatting of Lot 1, Block 2 and Lot 1, Block 3. 
There is a sliver of land that is between proposed Wild Bluff an Converse Avenue right-of-
way. The applicant will need to acquire an easement from the adjacent property owner 
prior to signing of the plat to make sure the connection will happen.    
 
Open Space (4.4): 
The applicant has requested that open space be addressed at a later date. The reasons they 
gave are as follows, Lot 1, Block 1 will have a site plan which will then address the open 
space requirement, Lot 1, Block 2 and Lot 1, Block 3 will either be replatted in the future or 
they too will have site plans. Staff is satisfied with the applicants request as open space 
requirements will be required either with a site plan or when a replat is submitted.  
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. 

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any commercial building or 
residential building on any of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees 
are payable at the issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with the study provided. 
 
Traffic: 
A traffic study was submitted and reviewed. There is some follow-up information requested.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
This plat is proposing three buildable lots. Lot 1, Block 1 is proposed to be zone for commercial 
and the proposed lot size would be a buildable lot for that zone district. Lot 1, Block 2 and Lot 1, 
Block 3 are for future residential development. They could be used for multi-family at the 
proposed size if they were to get conditional use approval or they could be replatted into single-
family lots at a later date. 
 
All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
 

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
Tract A is proposed to be a non-buildable lot. Note #5 on the plat indicates that it would be 
replatted in the future.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development. The plat 
proposes two additional major street connections and three buildable lots. Two of the lots are 
proposed for future residential development which is similar to what is in the area as well.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Tract A is proposed to be a non-buildable lot. Note #5 on the plat indicates that it would be 
replatted in the future.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommends minor 
corrections as conditions to the plat. These corrections should be resolved prior to final plat 
approval / signing of the mylars.  

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections (corrected with revised submittal). 
2. Some traffic concerns and road concerns (Partially corrected with revised submittal). 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant.  
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
 

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following conditions: 
1. Prior to Governing Body approval: 
a. Updated traffic information be provided and approved by the Engineering Department. 
2. Prior to Mylars being signed: 
a. An easement across Townsend Place Apartments LP be recorded to allow Wild Bluff to 
connect into Converse Avenue. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat
